History
During the events of the death curse in the late 15 century DR, Siela was one of several citizens of Malar's Throat to be captured by a group of pterafolk who attacked the district. She realized that she was to be sacrificed to the jungle, and was placed as an offering in a cluster of thorny brambles alongside a tabaxi named Ashen Rock. Despite her frantic calls for help, she was dragged screaming into the brambles and killed by twig and needle blights.

## Silverhand Tower

Silverhand Tower was the primary residence of Dornal Silverhand in the mid–8 century DR. It was also the birthplace of the Seven Sisters.

Location
The tower stood in a wooded dell near the city of Neverwinter and nearer the site of would later be the location of Longsaddle.

Structure
Silverhand Tower was little more than a cottage attached to an empty ring of battlements on a collapsed keep by the 760s DR. There was a small vegetable garden nearby, and presumably a stream and a stump both visible from the cottage.

History
After Dornal Silverhand and Elué Shundar got married, they moved to Silverhand Tower, where Dornal's senile mother welcomed Elué as the "sorceress in the moonlight" of her dreams ere passing away. Dornal and Elue lived in the tower where they had a new daughter every year, starting with Anastra Syluné in  and Alustriel in .

In the, Dornal, upon learning his wife's transformation into something ever closer to a lich was due to possession by some entity of overwhelming power, murdered his wife and fled the lands. Their six daughters were orphaned, which prompted Mystra to send her Chosen, Elminster Aumar, to look after the little girls.

When the elves of New Sharandar arrived in Neverwinter seeking aid circa 1479 DR, Silwen coordinated efforts to send adventurers to aid them.

## Silent Antlers

"Silent Antlers" was the name Elminster Aumar gave a mysterious entity he struck down in the  at a hill near Tresset's Ringyl.The name is used in capitals in the narration, but it is only from Elminster's perspective. The name is not ever spoken to refer to this mysterious entity.

Description
The bizarre creature was stag-headed and bare, with wickedly long fingers. Its eyes were red.

Personality
While Silent Antlers didn't say much, it gave Elminster the impression it was bent on killing him.

Abilities
The creature's attack was heralded by the rush of a throng of shadow-like beasts of unknown nature; it seemed to reabsorb them later, strongly implying that they were created from it in some manner.

It cast a magical green ball lightning, which could be rebounded. It could also take the lightning itself harmlessly, seemingly being resistant to spells or to the lightning itself. It had some ability to respond to spells that created daggers, dispelling or otherwise cancelling them. Finally, it had a spell that allowed it to release blue flames from its hands, which was also a form of counterspell capable of breaching other spell-shields quite effectively. It seemed to have no defense of its own towards that attack.

History
On a spring day of the, the Chosen of Mystra Elminster Aumar spent a night on a hill near Tresset's Ringyl, the ruin of a fortified town. He was attacked through the night by a number of shadowy beasts, which were followed by an unknown, speechless creature that Elminster dubbed "Silent Antlers". After a short spell-duel, he managed to slay it.

Upon death, the creature's body crumbled to ash; whether this was because of the rebounded flame spell it cast or some inherent property of its body was unclear. Its passing also caused a change in the Ringyl, a nearby castle, making a catafalque and casket burst out from behind a wall decorated with a carving of a pair of lips, which held 12 or 14 irregular stars around it. A rod that formed part of the arrangement then crumbled into dust, but was reclaimed and restored by the hand of an unseen woman, the sorceress Dasumia.

## Sina'zith

Sina'zith was a githyanki monk who died in the 15 century DR, killed as a result of her interaction with the Cult of the Absolute.

Description
Sina'zith was a lanky gith woman with yellowish coloration and gray splotches on her skin. She had blue eyes and light brown hair, braided in tight cornrows.

History
Sina'zith was an outcast among her kin, owing to her dedication to a certain monastic tradition. In the, she came into contact with the Cult of the Absolute and was infected with their altered illithid tadpole. When Sina'zith's fellow githyanki found out about the infection, she was slain immediately as an aberration. Her soul yearned for revenge and ended up among the hand-picked dozen individuals plucked from the Fugue Plane, chosen by the entity called Withers to aid in his campaign against the Dead Three and their Cult of the Absolute.

## Silas Silvertongue

Silas Silvertongue was a red cap boatswain of the Blushing Mermaid in the city of Baldur's Gate in the late 15 century DR.

In the, during the time Captain Grisly was replaced by the green hag Auntie Ethel as the owner of Blushing Mermaid, Silas Silvertongue resided at the Mermaid, alongside other hag's crewmen, spending their days drinking and partying. Silas was masquerading as a human sailor, keeping his fey nature a secret. It was unclear if the Silas Silvertongue identity was stolen from a slain sailor or was a fabrication.

## Silvertor

Silvertor was among the oldest noble houses of Waterdeep in the early 14 century DR, but as of 1368 DR it was no longer considered nobility.No other source on Waterdeep, such as City of Splendors, lists the Silvertors amongst the Waterdhavian nobility, suggesting they disappeared, likely by the time of Waterdeep and the North, 1347 DR.

History
In the, the young Lord Corin Silvertor had recently assumed the title after his father's passing, when he was lost on a hunting trip into Undermountain below Waterdeep. The half-orc thief Artek Ar'talen, called 'the Knife', was hired by Lord Darien Thal to rescue Lord Silvertor from Undermountain, and with the help of the wizard Beckla Shadesar, they located the young nobleman, and the three of them battled their way through Undermountain.

Members
 Corin Silvertor: Lord of House Silvertor as of 1325 DR.
 Lord Silvertor: The deceased patriarch of the family who died shortly before 1325 DR.
 Corlus Silvertor: The eldest brother of Corin Silvertor, who died of red fever prior to 1325 DR.
 Cordair Silvertor: The elder brother of Corin Silvertor, who was murdered for cheating at dice prior to 1325 DR.
 Lady Silvertor: The mother of Corin Silvertor, who died giving birth to him.

## Silaha Mnunzi

Silaha Mnunzi was a weapon buyer in service to the merchant prince Ekene-Afa of Port Nyanzaru in Chult during the late 15 century DR.

Personality
She was brusque in her business dealings.

Activities
Silaha acquired weapons on behalf of her merchant prince, who by law controlled all trade in weapons within Port Nyanzaru. This meant that foreign weapons dealers or blacksmiths were advised to seek her out and impress her if they wished to sell their wares in the city.

Silaha imported a great deal of weapons from lands north of Chult. She also worked alongside the Master of the Market to seek out skilled or notable smiths who visited Port Nyanzaru.

History
During the events of the death curse, Silaha was seeking a smith to produce axes, lances, and swords of titansteel.

## Silverleaf Halfmoon

Silverleaf Halfmoon was an adventuring elf in the Dalelands in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Description
He was blond and rugged-looking.

Abilities
Silverleaf was a competent warrior, and wielded wizardly abilities as well.

Possessions
Of note, he carried a bow and a quarterstaff.

Activities
He was an adventurer.

Relationships
Silverleaf was from the same tribe as Taran Goldenstar.

In Freedale, he was part of an adventuring party with Beldar, Delvar Ironfist, Morganth, Slinker, and Lordan.

Silverleaf Halfmoon was part of an adventuring group in Freedale that may have rescued Taran Goldenstar, recovered the orb of dragonkind, faced Gunter Weiss in Harrow Hill, and perhaps explored Azazabus' dungeon under Mount Dread.

Silverleaf Halfmoon is one of the sample adventurers provided for play with Introduction to Advanced Dungeons & Dragons. An identical sheet was introduced in First Quest. The Sigil of the Nine was a wondrous artifact from the city of Neverwinter that summoned the deceased members of the Neverwinter Nine to the Jewel of the North's defense in the times of need.

Powers
When the Sigil of the Nine was activated, the souls of the Neverwinter Nine entered the battlefield, helming a vicious all-out attack, all while boosting the morale of the caster and their allies, increasing their attack and defense for a short period of time.

History
The Sigil of the Nine was used by the heroes of the Siege of Neverwinter in the late 15 century DR in defense of their home against enemy hordes and against the Cloaked Ascendancy.

## Silk (drow)

Silk was a rebellious drow leader of a famous all-female adventuring troupe of seven – the Company of the Swanmays, active in the mid–14 century DR.

Description
Silk was a drow woman with her black skin tinted with purple and her black hair – streaked with silver. She was very young for an elf. Like other Swanmays, Silk bore the group's mark – the tattoo of a stylized swan on the back of the hand.

Personality
Silk had a commanding presence and called herself a rebel.

History
Much of Silk's personal life and history before joining the Swanmays remained a mystery. Under her leadership, the adventuring company often adventured around Suzail, the Dalelands, and surrounding regions. When they earned their living by guarding and guiding trader caravans through Cormanthor, they often passed through Shadow Gap and stayed in the North Gate Inn. When seasons changed, they spent their winters in Shadowdale, enjoying the warm company and strong drinks with Jhaele Silvermane at the Old Skull Inn.

Even though Alias' false memories suggested the adventuring group to be long-disbanded, they were very much still active, as in the, Silk and the Swanmays aided the adventuring party possessing the Azure Bonds to kill Dracandros, a Red Wizard who took over the village of Hap in Cormyr. Silk encountered the said group of adventurers in Hap and offered them a reward for slaying one of Dracandros's black dragons and acquiring a dragon's egg from a dracolich's lair. Were the adventurers female, Silk would offer them a membership in the Swanmays. However, it was unknown if they accepted the offer.

A few years later, the Swanmays found themselves captured by a group of drow after they spent a night at Zhentil Keep. The drow occupied the city, holding hostage some of the citizens and all the Swanmays members but Silk. To the residents of Zhentil Keep's dismay, the drow were allowed to run amok as the leaders of the keep turned a blind eye to it all. The reason behind the abductions was that one of Bane's three lieutenants, the marilith Kaliste, was using the abductees as sacrifices. Silk managed to recruit heroes from Phlan to aid her in the rescue mission into the depths of Manshoon's dungeon. Silk and adventurers discovered that the goodly hostages were siphoned off of their life force to create the essence of pure light, used to transform giant spiders into grotesque and powerful pets of Kalistes. One of the missing party members of Silk's was Cynthia, who was forced into magical servitude by Kaliste.

Appearances

## Silent (drow)

Silent was a drow agent who investigated Graz'zt's schemes in Tethyamar during the 1480s DR.

Description
Silent had a large white tattoo on his face, wore a suit of plate and carried twin scimitars.

Personality
He was quite restrained and careful, and avoided needless bloodshed. He also did not worship Lolth.

Abilities
Silent was a skilled and deadly warrior. He was mute, and knew a form of sign language with which to communicate. When with those who did not know his language, he had to rely on gestures and pantomime, or his companion's telepathy.

Relationships
He was quite fond of his friend and fellow agent Zoraxzil, and served as his bodyguard.

History

When Graz'zt began causing chaos and destruction with his jewels of ardor in Tethyamar, a third party hired Silent and Zoraxzil to investigate and find the source of the problem. Although they managed to track down Graz'zt's lair, they found themselves trapped in the stereotypical illusion of Faraway, being menaced by demons disguised as human villagers. Silent and Zoraxzil were separated, and a band of (hopefully) cunning heroes shortly after entered the illusion in pursuit of Graz'zt, who had taken the form of Silent just before disappearing. To make things worse, the false villagers told the newcomers that Silent was a murderous drow invader set on destroying the village. Though his fate seemed dark, Silent may have been able to use hand gestures to signal his non-aggression and work with the others to foil Graz'zt's flat and uninspired storytelling and railroaded plot.

## Sirocco (tribe)

The Sirocco was a tribe of aarakocra based in Anauroch in north Faerûn. Sirocco aarakocra were more slender and ferocious than other aarakocra.

Organization
A shaman held a position of importance within the Sirocco tribe. The shamans passed on secrets to their successors.

Activities
The Sirocco tribe ate meat that they hunted and scavenged for. They did not eat creatures with intelligence, but did eat animals from caravans that passed through Anauroch.

The Sirocco tribe had an elaborate funerary ritual. When one of them died, the body was flown to the Mesa of the Fallen, a part of their territory that only their shaman was allowed to visit. The shaman performed a secret rite involving chanting, drumming, and placing feathers of family members onto the dead body. Then the shaman took the body into the Cave of Beyond in the Mesa of the Fallen. Unknown to the aarakocra, the cave was a portal to some other place, activated by the final part of the funeral rite. When the body disappeared from it, it was actually being taken elsewhere. The aarakocra believed that the spirit of the deceased was taken to a place called the land of Open Sky.

Thus, they carried a superstition associating caves with the dead.

Base of Operations
The Sirocco aarakocra's territory encompassed a number of mesas, on which they lived. One mesa in particular was the Mesa of the Fallen, which was on the edge of their territory. Only the shaman of the tribe visited the Mesa of the Fallen. Inside was the Cave of Beyond, a portal to elsewhere.

History
The shaman of the Sirocco tribe was captured by stingers circa 1372 DR. The aaracokra were unable to pursue the stingers because of their fear of death as without their shaman they would not be able to conduct the secret funerary ritual. The son of the shaman was chosen by the chief, after a series of long councils. His task was to find people from outside the tribe that could save their shaman for them. His delegation visited Silverymoon, and attempted to make an agreement with Alustriel Silverhand.

## Sight link

Sight link was a minor psionic devotion of the telepathy discipline that allowed a psionicist to connect another creature's sense of sight to their own.

Effects
This power tapped into the optical system of another creature from an unlimited distance away, making the manifester see whatever the linked creature saw. In some cases, this also established a link with the target's sense of hearing. This power had no adverse effects on the manifester's own sense of sight. If the manifestation backfired, the manifester would be blinded for 1 to 4 hours.

If the linked creature was subjected to a gaze-based ability or effect, such as the petrifying gaze of a medusa, the manifester could also be affected.

Prerequisite Powers
In order to learn sight link a manifester first had to know the powers contact and mindlink.

Users
Sight link was found to manifest as an innate psionic ability by emerald dragons. And while not typically a wild talent, sight link sometimes manifested as such for necromancers, whom were known for developing unusual mental abilities.

Psicrystals could similarly enable a sight link with their owner, though only for a period of 1 hour per day at a distance of no more than .

Known/Notable Users
 Nar'Lheeth, an illithid of the Tamer Creed in the city of Nar'Vheen.
 The Watchers, illithids that served as the early warning system for the Underdark community of Nar'Vheen, included sight link among their repertoire of powers and used it to spy from afar.

## Sisters Three Waxworks

The Sisters Three Waxworks was a shop in the Central Trade Lanes of Skullport in the late 14 century DR.

Structure
The Sisters Three Waxworks was a one-story building in fair to poor condition.

Interior
The shop's front room was dominated by candles of all shapes and sizes, arranged across every available surface. More unsettling were the lifelike wax statues of men and women placed upon shelves and pedestals, their blank expressions fostering rumors that they were, in fact, wax golems. It was whispered that these creations could animate should the sisters ever be threatened.

Beyond a beaded curtain was the workshop, where wax was melted and molded. This area contained an iron stove, cauldrons and pans, some filled with melted wax, alongside neatly stacked blocks of wax, bottles of scented extracts, dried herbs, braided wicks, and other tools of the trade.

Services
The Sisters Three Waxworks specialized in the manufacture of candles of various sizes, colors, and scents. Basic unscented tapers were sold at standard prices, but colored or scented varieties, beyond those derived from beeswax or rendered fat, were more expensive, reflecting their superior quality (average to high prices, excellent craftsmanship). Beyond candles, the shop created intricate wax effigies of people, animals, and arcane symbols, catering to both artistic and magical needs. Additionally, they sold an array of folk remedies, including herbal cures, tonics, balms, and elixirs made from a variety of natural ingredients such as herbs, crystals, and fungi.

Inhabitants
Despite the shop's singular name, visitors were often surprised to discover that it was managed by three individuals, the identical sisters Kaitlynn, Briglynn, and Anithlynn. Each possessed a distinct personality and approach to their craft.

## Silvenius Crim

Silvenius Crim was a wizard agent of the Arcane Brotherhood of the Host Tower of the Arcane and the proprietor of Icewind Enchantments in the town of Targos in Icewind Dale in the late 14 century DR.

Description
Silvenius was a pale-skinned human man with wavy chestnut hair, a sour expression, and the posture of a powerful mage. He preferred transportation through magic to walking.

History
In the, Silvenius Crim was present at the Host Tower of the Arcane when it was stormed by the Hero of Neverwinter and their companions. The mage witnessed the adventurers cut down members of the Arcane Brotherhood who fell to Maugrim Korothir's mental domination, including Valindra Shadowmantle, who was killed by the Hero. Silvenius survived, but the experience left him traumatized. Soon after, Silvenius became aware that the Archmage Arcane had returned his favorite wizard, Valindra back to life, believing that she possessed some grand destiny. Silvenius admitted that the Hero of Neverwinter saved the Host Tower of the Arcane despite killing many of its mages.

By Mirtul of the, when the Ten Towns were facing looming destruction by an ancient evil, Silvenius Crim had recently arrived from Luskan and opened Icewind Enchantments, a shop that dealt in magical goods. This venture was not Silvenius's favorite assignment, and he could not stand the backwater town and its coinless inhabitants, but such was his punishment for deserting the Host Tower during the Hero of Neverwinter's assault. Secretly, Silvenius Crim was tasked with manning Icewind Enchantments by his superiors at the Host Tower of the Arcane. He was ordered to perform research in the Art and to seek "economic opportunities."

One such magical research included performing a complex and dangerous ritual of summoning a snow weird. The wizard was to perform the ritual using rare materials provided to him by Luskan. Silvenius was to ask the elemental creature, a font of esoteric knowledge and prognostication of the future, about one topic to pass the knowledge back to the Hosttower. To help him with the ritual as bodyguards, Silvenius Crim acquired the help of the Hero of Neverwinter, who was promised a question of their own. The mage recognized the Hero but he did not consider them an enemy as he cared about magical research more than he cared for the Arcane Brotherhood's goals.

When the snow weird was summoned, Silvenius presented his first query: who or what was threatening the Ten Towns? The weird begged the wizard to be returned back to the elemental pool of snow she was ripped away from, as a snow weird could never survive without it. Nevertheless, the weird answered the mage's question: the ancient demigod of war and winter, Arctus, was about to break out from its prison and lay waste to Icewind Dale. The snow weird raged and begged in pain to be released. Silvenius's second query was at the behest of the Host Tower: what was Valindra Shadowmantle's destiny? The snow weird went berserk in desperation and attacked, yelling the prophesy of Valindra becoming the Scourge of Neverwinter. The Hero was never given an opportunity to ask their question. Still, together with their allies, the group ended up either slaying the desperate elemental or destroying the summoning circle and sending the creature back to the Elemental Plane of Air. As payment for their aid, Silvenius paid the Hero in gold coin. Eventually, Silvenius Crim may have offered aid to the Hero and the Targos Guard when the city was attacked by Arctus and its army. On orders from Speaker Kemp, Silvenius lit huge magical braziers around Targos, offering its people protection against Arctus's deadly dire blizzard.

## Silver slinger

The Silver slinger was a holy repeating heavy crossbow found in Eberron during the mid-1480s DR off Faerûn.

The crossbow was carved in the form of a beaked, horned beast, with wings outstretched serving as the prods and bolts shooting from its mouth.

Powers
The crossbow was filled with holy and good energy, making it exceptional at smiting evil. It was even more deadly against the undead, and each bolt shot from it was magically enhanced as if they were made of silver, better harming monsters like lycanthropes and vampires, and further altered so that they struck with crushing rather than piercing force, letting them shatter skeletons easily.

## Silverfin

Silverfin was a term for varieties of fish found in the waters of Faerûn.

Availability
Silverfin were common in the rivers and oceans of Faerûn.

Central Faerûn
Large schools of whitefish were encountered in Sea of Fallen Stars, and the waters of the Dragonmere and the Neck that lay between Cormyr, Sembia, and the Dragon Coast.

Silverfin were also found in the Wyvernwater in Cormyr, and were a common catch of the 'mist fishers' of Immersea. In Immersea's fishpacking plants, silverfin were preserved in salt or oil and stored in kegs for transport and sale both within and beyond Cormyr. Silverfin and other fish were cultivated in the fishponds of the Royal Court in Suzail for meals at the Court and the Palace of the Purple Dragon, and in the mansions of Arabel.

Silverfin were also caught in the Moonsea. In cities like Hulburg on the north shore, Moonsea silverfins were cured in smoking-houses and there sold to traders. Moonsea silverfins would then be traded by fishmonger's wagons inland to places like Voonlar.

Sea of Swords and the Sword Coast
Silverfin was common in the Sea of Swords. Villages on the Sword Coast south of the Cloak Wood caught these drag-nets from small fishing boats. On the island of Mintarn in the Sea of Swords, tiny whitefish were farmed in breeding pools and fed with kitchen scraps.

They also caught by the fishermen of Kalathtyr, Amn, which lay at the mouth of the River Specie where it flowed into the Sea of Swords.It is unclear whether these whitefish are caught from the river or the sea, and therefore whether they are freshwater or saltwater.

Silverfin, both saltwater and freshwater, were the most common seafood on offer in Waterdeep's fish markets, all year around. Felhaur's Fine Fish was a Waterdhavian fishmonger's that sold silverfin, among others.

Western Heartlands
The River Chionthar was another source of silverfin, and they were fished around Berdusk and Scornubel.

The North
In the Silver Marches, silverfin were almost the species found in the River Rauvin, River Surbrin, and lesser rivers of the region. The majority of human travelers here could tell a silverfin from a bluefin or other fish, and how to net or cook one simply.

Usage
Culinary

Silverfin was the most common kind of fish served in Faerûnian cuisine, particularly along the Sword Coast. It was also a feature of dwarven cuisine. It was eaten in a variety of dishes:
 A traveler could cook a silverfin or other fish simply by putting it in a skillet over a fire.
 The namesake silverfin species was considered best cooked by pan-frying in wine, brandy, or ale, but was typically rolled in mud with butterweed or wild onion or garlic, and baked in a fire. They were also preserved in oil.
 Roast silverfin was a common feature of the eveningfeast menu in respectable inns and restaurants in coastal towns and cities.
 Bowls of pickled silverfin and other small fish were often on the eveningfeast menu in high-class eateries, or might be eaten as a basic morningfeast.
 Spiced silverfin was among the spreads served on biscuits at the Elfstone Tavern in Waterdeep.
 Silverfin daggers—slivers of fish on lemon or lime slices—were served as canapés at The Gentle Mermaid in Waterdeep.
 Smoked silverfin was served in a buffet at morningfeast at the Palace of Waterdeep for Piergeiron Paladinson and guests.
 In Waterdeep, businesses sold 'coldsear silverfin', which was grilled silverfin meat frozen in ice and delivered to customers for defrosting and consumption.
 Stuffed silverfin was served at Firefall Keep, seat of the Summerstar family of Cormyr, in 1365 DR.
 Crusted silverfin cheese was a smallbite served at functions in Cormyr.
 Smoked silverfin on skewers, locally caught, were commonly served at the Axefall club in Scornubel.
 Plate of Silver was a dish of grilled silverfin drizzled with raspberry juice and vinegar served at Stargath House in Purskul, Amn.
 Lightly smoked silverfin in a cream broth with fried yellow peppers, mushrooms, and asparagus was served at Blackthorn's Brew in Velen, Tethyr.
 Barrels of silverfin in sauce were kept as provision in Shadowdark House in Daerlun.
 Butter-fried Moonsea silverfin was a seasonal treat in Voonlar.

Arcane
An eye from a silverfin fish caught with a silver fishhook was once used as a random ingredient included in the tincture used in the shell creation stage of item enchantment in order to introduced deliberate randomness to a magic item.

Species

Some particular types of fish were considered silverfin:
Silverfin Known simply as "silverfin", this namesake species had silvery scales on their sides and silvery fins with black roots that effectively highlighted their appearance, and grew to the size of trout. They were abundant, lived in freshwater, saltwater, and brackish water, and they consumed insects, worms, small eels, and frogs. Hence, they were easy to catch by even amateur fishers, even with nets or baskets. They had a taste like bass and kept well in oil.
Blindfin Blindfin were a voracious subterranean fish found in pools in Downshadow in Undermountain. These were mutated from silverfin by the Spellplague.Undermountain: Halaster's Lost Apprentice p. 16 features a hazardous fish as "silverfin" and refers to Forgotten Realms Campaign Guide p. 199, which actually discusses a fish called "blindfin". It's possible the blindfin was intended but misnamed, but The God Catcher discusses blindfin being mutated silverfin.
Luslar A blind species of fish very similar to Sword Coast silverfin that swam in Lake Thalmiir in the Underdark. These were fished by the Sschindylryn drow and preyed on by predatory eels.
Trout Some kinds of trout were considered silverfin, at least in dwarven cuisine.

Appendix
Background
While commonly mentioned in many sources before and since, Dragon #277: "The New Adventures of Volo: Dragonwing Stew" by Ed Greenwood (2000) finally defines silverfin as "any whitefish". However, this term has multiple meanings. First, whitefish covers a variety of bottom-feeding fish with pale flesh and low oil content that live in seas and lakes. Alternatively, whitefish is the Coregoninae subfamily of lake-dwelling fish. As such, "silverfin" encompasses a range of potential fish that may be detailed more specifically elsewhere, such as cod, flounder, haddock, halibut, hake, plaice, pollock, sole, and whiting, as well as various flatfish, in the former case, and ciscoes and whitefishes in the latter case.

Further complicating the question of 'what is a silverfin?', in a 2019 tweet, Ed Greenwood described the silverfin in terms that implied a particular species of fish, rather than a grouping. For the purposes of this article, this is presumed to be a common, representative, or namesake species of the group. In a 2022 tweet and 2025 Patreon/YouTube video on dwarven cuisine, he named silverfin a trout instead; this is presumed to be a type of silverfin under the earlier definition as per our understandng of canon, though on Earth trout is not considered a whitefish. Maybe dwarves don't know any better.

## Silvermoon Playhouse

The Silvermoon Playhouse was a theater in the city of Ravens Bluff that had been constructed around either 1370 or 1371 DR.

Structure
This elegant building was directly connected to Darksilver House, the grand mansion of the swashbuckling adventurer Lorien Darkarrow, who had financed the building's construction and continued to act as its financier. Situated on the exterior was a ticket office, where customers paid for seating. And the antechamber's roof was a clear crystal, providing a view of the moon at night.

Interior
The interior of the playhouse was gilded with golden trim and polished silver. Its ante-chamber was notably a large bar, measuring  in length. The building's backdoor lead into Lorien's home.

Services
The Silvermoon Playhouse was owned and operated by a theater troupe of Selûnite adventurers, who typically put on very tasteful plays of marked quality. The playhouse was closed during the day, as its plays usually began at moonrise or two hours before sunrise. Plays usually began and end with a small, non-overbearing selection of scenes that constituted worship of Selûne, acting as the troupe's gift to their goddess. It was also not uncommon for many in the audience to be encouraged to sing along to its musical numbers.

The price of tickets varied depending upon seating, but were kept relatively cheap, owing to the ownership situation and the playhouse's financier. Seats in the upper balcony cost 5 gold pieces, seats on the main level cost 10 gp, and a ticket for seats close to the orchestra cost 25 gp.

Whenever a show wasn't being hosted, the playhouse often opened its private bar to the public. The varied menu of the bar included The Moon's Head Brace for 15 gp, Silverymoon Ale for 10 gp, The Silver Moonbeam for 50 gp, and The Walking Furniture 20 gp.

Defenses
The playhouse rarely suffered from thievery, as it was protected by an array of guards and wards and Mordenkainen's faithful hound, both spells that had been put up by Lorien Darkarrow. Additionally, being that it was owned by Lorien Darkarrow, who was both a landowner and knight of Ravens Bluff, the City Guard took care to apprehend anyone attempting to break into the Silvermoon.

Inhabitants
While a majority of performers at the Silvermoon were part of the playhouse's theater troupe, many of its other bards were visiting friends of Lorien who acted for free, performing simply for the love of the theater. There were initially tensions over this, but once the troupe understood their livelihoods weren't at risk they began to have more fun with the proceedings.

The leader of the Silvermoon's theater troupe was the half-elf bard Yvandolyn. Doris, a half-elven werebear specialty priest of Selûne, operated the ticket office and guarded the main entrance during the day until noon, when the playhouse's large cleaning staff clocked in. And its bar was managed by Michael and Stanley, who were Selûnite laymen that took pride in being able to work at Silvermoon.

History
Within its first six tendays of operation, the Silvermoon Playhouse had performed four distinct plays. Its third performance went down rather poorly, so much so that the audience were vacating the playhouse and a halfling backrow shouted a demand for a refund. The staff obliged this request and began returning the coins of theatergoers. However, a visiting bardic master friends of Lorien by the name of Morgan gathered together a number of singers to the stage and told the audience to forget what they had been watching before, proclaiming that a brand new play was going to be performed that evening. After a half-bell intermission, this new play was performed and it won over the affection of the theatergoers. Such fervor was generated over this new play that for the next thirty days tickets for it were sold out.

On their sixth week of operation, the Silvermoon Playhouse began advertising its newest show, which involved pirates, shipwrecks and a dragon turtle. Tickets for the show's premiere were sold at five times their normal value.

## Sindarian

## Sineater

Sineater was an immense purple worm that dwelled in the Underdark beneath the Cay'Vorym Fighting Pit in the 1480s DR.

Description
Sineater was a truly massive example of a purple worm, far larger and stronger than any others. It had a vibrant internal ecosystem of ooze-like digestive slimes, parasitic leeches, and scavenger vermin that thrived in its cavernous guts.

History

During the Sschindylryn invasion of Eveningstar, House Cay'Vorym operated the Cay'Vorym Fighting Pit, where Feryl Cay'Vorym ran bloodsports between gladiators, slaves, and monsters. The house took advantage of the war to take captured Purple Dragons and use them for entertainment. When Sorina Parnock sent a team from Eveningstar to rescue them, Feryl dropped them into the arena and forced them to fight for their lives, culminating in him summoning up purple worms from deeper underground. While an extraordinary show, the wild monsters destroyed the arena stands and devoured the spectators in the process, culminating in Sineater breaking through the floor, eating Feryl, and collapsing the entire arena. The Cormyrean band fell down into a great cavern below the area—filled with enormous unhatched worm eggs—and after a brief battle they too were swallowed.

The Cormyreans fought their way through the guts of Sineater as they were slowly digested by stomach acid, finding fragments of the shattered Earthcarver as they went. Along the way they overcame a group of confused and desperate yuan-ti, various digestive threats, and finally found Feryl and his drow companions. After a climactic battle Feryl was slain and the last pieces of Earthcarver put together, then they used it to carve a hole in the side of Sineater and escape. While Sineater was reeling from the mortal wound, the Cormyreans performed one final assault and finally slew the great beast, leaving its remains among the eggs in the cavern.

Sigurd Helmudson ruled over the whole of the realm of Gnarhelm from his capital in the city of Gnarhelm.

History
Circa, King Helmudson and his soldiers unintentionally left the city of Garhelm undefended, leading to a raid by pirates who seemed intent on razing the town. However, the city was saved by the fortunate intervention of King Thelgaar Ironhand of Oman and King Grunnarch the Red of Norland, who arrived by longship while traveling with Elminster just as the raid began. ## Singing Rocks

The Singing Rocks was a tiny archipelago in the Sea of Swords or Trackless Sea off the coast of the Lands of Intrigue in west Faerûn.

Geography
The rocks rose from the sea due west of the midway point between Memnon in Calimshan and Myratma in Tethyr, so it, or rather its northernmost end, marked the sea border between the two realms. Moreover, captains commonly used the Singing Rocks as a vital aid to navigation in order to avoid going too far off course to the west, away from the safety of the coast. However, this was less of a concern by 1370 DR.

The majority of storms on the Sea of Swords were expended well before they reached the Singing Rocks, and the most fierce broke up on the rocks themselves. Thus the port cities of Calimshan did not enjoy strong sea breezes as found in the north, while the coastal waters were calm.

One of the outer isles was called Tonter.

The remote, lonely, and tiny hermitage island of Falconsrise was even further west of the Singing Rocks, and rarely even marked on maps.

Description
The rocks were named for a strange effect reported by sailors whose ships had ventured close by some time before 1357 DR, claiming to have heard singing from hundreds of feminine voices. They described it as beautiful, elusive, and haunting. However, it apparently had no dangerous charming effects, as was the case in harpy or siren song. Moreover, landing parties found that the singing stopped around  from the shores and that, when they landed, there was no living creature, magical effect, or any other source for the sound. Although detect magic sensed the presence of magic, other forms of magical detection failed to give any more detailed information, as if the divination was blocked by something.

History
The Bloody Buccaneers often hunted in the vicinity of the Singing Rocks circa 1370 DR with their two cutters, Umberlee's Wake and the Water Weird.

Around that time, a kraken (or krakens) known as the Curse of Irphong commanding a force of merrow, sahuagin, and scrag led its forces into the seas amidst the Singing Rocks and Nelanther Isles. Beneath the waves, they demolished settlements or conscripted aquatic peoples into their swelling army.

Inhabitants
The ancient white dragon Arveiaturace had, on occasion, roosted on the isle of Tonter whilst on her far-reaching hunts.

But an even more dangerous occupant of the Singing Rocks was Shond Tharovin, the wizard and vizar of Schamedar cursed into the form of an enormous gibbering mouther, last seen drifting out to sea in 1367 DR. Evidently, he washed up on the Singing Rocks and made them his lair. Shond could cover more than  of one of the isles or, by stretching his amorphous form along the seabed to up to, could extend across nearly all the northern isles. The chemicals exuded by his aberrant body also repelled most predators. As such, it's possible he had some contact with the krakens, though he would seek another powerful magical relic, Kayas the Krakenscourge, to manipulate the Curse of Irphong and other krakens.

## Silas Morgenstern

Silas Morgenstern was the owner of the General Store in Stag Falls during the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Possessions
He owned the general store in town.

History
After the vampire Romanisk Vlaensk turned Baron Vilhelm von Stag Falls into his undead servant, Silas Morgenstern fled with the rest of the villagers into the nearby hills. He tried to return to his general store once for supplies, but the baron's guards forced him to escape empty-handed.

## Sidone

Sidone was a druid of Chauntea in Stag Falls during the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Abilities
She was a druid.

Possessions
She had a ring of spell storing, with several dispel magic spells on it.

Activities
Sidone was a Chauntean priest in Stag Falls.

History
After the vampire wizard Romanisk Vlaensk turned Baron Vilhelm von Stag Falls into his undead thrall, and in turn the Baron began to terrorize the town with his charmed retinue, Sidone led the population of Stag Falls to nearby caves, where they hid and took refuge. Her fellow priest, a Tormite called Gunther, did not care to join them, and made a last stand; she feared the worst for him, and correctly so.

Help finally arrived as some adventurers were sent from Ravens Bluff to investigate, as the taxes from Stag Falls were late. Welcome in Sidone's camp at the nearby caves after aiding Tommy Fletcher, a shepherd boy of the same town, the adventurers spent a night resting; on that night, Sidone searched the neighboring region for two youths, Kitarno and Lyra, without much success. This caused her to miss the birth of the child of Jurgen and Marimir, two fellow Stag Falls villagers, whose baby had to be midwifed by the same adventurers until Sidone arrived. Before these adventurers finally headed for the castle, she gave them a ring of spell storing and goodberries.

After the destruction of Romanisk Vlaensk, Sidone was the person with the most support to take over Stag Falls, but she was not interested in leadership positions and would only put up with those temporarily.

## Silvenestri Silver

Silvenestri Silver was a gnome treasure hunter of Sembia in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Personality
Silver took pride in his job hunting for treasure. He loved to make trouble.

Abilities
He could employ illusions in some manner; the dwarf Badger Bates called him an illusionist and recalled an occasion where Silver claimed to cast an illusion. Silver himself claimed lesser gnomes couldn't have done what he did, strongly implying he was a mage of sorts. He did not, however, actually know much about dragons.

Possessions
He had an embroidered hankie.

Activities
Silvenestri Silver was a treasure-stealer who took dragons' hoards, sometimes as part of a team. He spent winters in smaller towns, away from the cities; he was especially fond of the Dragon Defeated tavern in Halfknot.

Relationships
Silver often antagonized his companions at work, who believed they'd been cheated of their share. He argued about dragons almost every night with two fellow tavern-goers at the Dragon Defeated: Wyrmbait Nix and Badger Bates.

History
Silvenestri Silver often told and retold a tale about a black dragon with a phobia for snakes, which strongly implied the entirety of his hoard-stealing business had consisted entirely of that one time.

In the mid-winter of the, the Dragon Defeated sign got repainted; the owner, Varney, had it redrawn to resemble Silver and two other regulars: Badger Bates and Wyrmbait Nix. They soon became local celebrities, "dragon experts", taking part in a contest every mid-tenday where they judged tales of dragons.

During the last such contest held, in late winter or early spring of that year, Silvenestri claimed green dragons were often cross-eyed and dull-witted in response to a tale he was judging. That provoked a response from the painter of the sign, Petra, who challenged him on that, and produced a huge number of paintings of such green dragons, all of which she claimed to have painted from life. He corrected himself as speaking about white dragons, to which Petra responded by revealing her paintings of white dragons.

Badger Bates dismissed her paintings for not containing any pictures of Malaeragoth. When she claimed to have seen Malaeragoth more recently than he did, Silver required her to show proof. She painted Malaeragoth on a wall of the tavern, but refused to draw his eyes, instead simply describing them. Bates refused to pay up unless the eyes were drawn.

Silver egged on Petra, siding with the dwarf just to make trouble. When her picture of Malaeragoth came to life, however, he had the good sense and reflexes to jump out the window and run for the edge of town; he returned to find Badger Bates flattened.

He immediately took the dwarf's wallet and scale.

## Sisyphus

Sisyphus was a mortal man imprisoned in the Underworld, the divine realm of Hades in Pluton or "Erebus" as some called it, for a perennial period. His name lived on as one of many dire legends in the multiverse, a warning against messing with the ever temperamental Olympian pantheon.

Activities
The eternal punishment that Sisyphus suffered within the Underworld was having roll a massive boulder up to the brow of a hill, and then topple it over. However, in every instance of him attempting this task, he would be overwhelmed by the boulder's weight at the last second and it would bounces back down. Thus was the legend of Sisyphus, forever condemned to repeat this process.

Any attempts made to ease his pain, even something as simple as giving him a drink, would draw the immediate attention and wrath of the deity Hades, for his suffering was but one part of a grander scheme.

History
At the request of his fellow Olympians, Hades kept Sisyphus a prisoner within his realm. He was guilty of a number of crimes, from robbery to murder, but the thoughtless act that condemned him to Pluton was betraying divine secrets of the gods and later tricking Hades into handcuffs.

In the, on the 65th layer of the Abyss, a balor general by the name of Raachaak had caught wind of the location of a fabled Torilian artifact in the Utter East, known as the bloodforge. He ordered a trio of subservient vrocks to that Prime Material world to capture the bloodforge for him, commenting that once he got his hands on the device, "I'll raise an army large enough to overrun stinking Baator in a single roll of the Sisyphus Stone!"

## Silver Flame Guardian

The Silver Flame Guardian was a fragment or avatar of the Silver Flame, a deity of Eberron. He was charged with containing the Spinner of Shadows in the Spinner's Prison below Stormreach.

Description
The Guardian appeared to be a blazing mass of silver-blue fire.

History

At some point in the distant past, a fragment of the goddess Lolth was trapped in Khyber, becoming one of the realm's fiendish Overlords. The Guardian was likewise a small piece of the Silver Flame, tasked with maintaining the prison and preventing the Spinner from escaping. He was joined at the Spinner's Prison in this by a series of mortal servants who maintained the wards and protections set upon the prison, ensuring they did not deteriorate or get sabotaged. The latest comrade in this was Brother Aurelius, a scholarly sort who regarded the Guardian as a colleague.

During the Darkening of mid-1480s DR in Faerûn, the Lords of Dust cult hidden in Stormreach sought to release the Spinner of Shadows. Finding their way into the underworld by the road to Tarath Marad, they broke through the Guardian's defenses and set about weakening the prison, including the use of magical spiders that devoured and weakened the Guardian's physical fiery form. Before the breakout could be completed, however, heroes sent by the Templar Orrex arrived and defeated the cultist, meeting with the Guardian before entering the prison themselves to face the Spinner. They destroyed the flame eater spiders that the Spinner was using to devour the Silver Flame and foiled her attempts to escape, only for Lolth to personally intervene. She broke open a Rift Between Worlds from the Demonweb and took the Spinner of Shadows into her domain, and only the efforts of the Guardian prevented the violent conjunction from killing the heroes.

Following this encounter, the Guardian bid the heroes continue their efforts, leading them into the Demonweb, meeting Elminster, and eventually finding the village of Eveningstar in Faerûn.

## Silver Flame

The Silver Flame was a deity local to the world of Eberron. An abstract being of law and good, the Silver Flame and its church were at the forefront of fighting against the forces of evil and chaos, particularly the fiendish Lords of Dust.

History
During the ancient Age of Demons, the world was ravaged by the fell Overlords, fiendish beings of immense power and cruelty. The Silver Flame trapped and held the Overlords in great prisons hidden in the underworld of Khyber, binding them with its own essence. The Lords of Dust sought endlessly to find and free the Overlords and usher in a new Age of Demons, but were countered by the Church of the Silver Flame.

Around the 1480s DR of Faerûn, the rakshasa Gnomon infiltrated the Church of the Silver Flame and managed to discover the Spinner's Prison, home of the great and terrible Spinner of Shadows. He put together a conspiracy to break open the road to Tarath Marad using a ritual and then undo the wards on the prison, allowing the Spinner to run free. Though his plans were only partially successful (the Spinner immediately betrayed the Lords of Dust), it was enough to overpower the Silver Flame Guardian and let the Spinner call out to Lolth for rescue. The Spinner departed through the Rift Between Worlds, leaving the forces of the Silver Flame to handle the aftermath while Lolth pursued the conquest of the Weave in Faerûn.

Notable Items
The agents and champions of the Silver Flame were equipped with various pieces of blessed wargear, including the Bow of the Silver Flame, Fury of the Flame, Luminous Truth, Silver slinger, Templar's bastion, Templar's bulwark, Templar's docent, Templar's Justice, and Templar's retribution.
